The similar absolute residual saving helps explain dilution over a longer forecast, but this residual includes initialization, final output/drain and MPI effects; it is not pure I/O or proof of a single cause. The 1.5× throughput and 2× speedup goals remain unmet; kernel, caller, three-hour and nine-hour gains are not added.

## Profiles and references

The full-grid 1500×1140 development profile attributes substantial startup time to initial output (96.42 s). Selected-rank steady samples identify positive-definite scalar advection (10.55%), ordinary scalar advection (5.66%), scalar update (3.92%), AFWA driver (3.26%) and RH calculation (1.88%). These are sampled self costs, not additive inclusive call-tree fractions or a complete critical-path attribution.

A subsequent 60-second, two-rank debug profile on the independently initialized 450×342 common case collected about 5,000 samples with zero loss. Its self costs include positive-definite advection 7.05%, vertical-velocity advancement 5.31%, ordinary advection 5.13%, Thompson microphysics 4.56%, AFWA driver 3.48% and RH calculation 1.38%. Domain and forecast phase differ; these percentages do not establish whole-stage gains.

The common case preserves 2.5 km spacing, 33 vertical levels, CONUS physics, adaptive timesteps and hourly output. It has its own physically generated initial/boundary inputs, not slices of a larger domain. The original six-hour run completed in 1,412.40 s; distributed execution took 1,634.31 s under shared development load. These are not authoritative scaling measurements.

The original protected scientific checker remains unchanged. An additive validator verifies layout, timestamps, inventory, restart files and first divergence; 18 mutation tests pass. Job-local per-variable read-cache management changes resource use, not the comparison predicate. Observed timestep equality uses the precision printed by WRF (two decimals); internal timestep-bit equality is unobserved.

## Correctness findings

`phys/module_diag_afwa.F` passes `terrain_dx` and `terrain_dy` to `LLT_MountainWave` without declarations, assignments or supplied arguments. A scan of 743 source files finds no other binding. The helper directly uses those values, and its result feeds precisely the four failed diagnostics. Controlled unchanged-helper tests demonstrate large changes in this four-field family when only explicit synthetic terrain coefficients change. This supports the defect diagnosis; it is not a capture proving the exact live values that caused every observed mismatch.

A separate cloud-ceiling typo uses undefined `ceil_min` instead of the declared 125 m `min_ceil`. A separate correction passes five boundary tests under bounds checks and O2. It is not included in any performance build.

The owner-approved separate terrain correction defines physical grid-aligned slopes using `MAPFAC_MX/MY` and `rdx/rdy`, centered interior differences, and one-sided physical mass-grid boundaries. The original has no defined stencil to preserve. Flat/linear, variable-metric, quadratic boundary and decomposition-window tests pass O0 bounds checks and strict O2. The complete AFWA module builds under the retained strict flags. Three actual independent DeepSeek reviews and their limitations are retained.

The corrected executable (`5729ec328e3df27909a0660534ddd780cba24bd58f3d1be900067e0af9cfab06`) passes stock-versus-zlib-ng all-field/metadata/layout exact comparisons at hours 0, 1 and 2; all 365 observed adaptive steps match. Legacy-versus-corrected hour 0 passes, hours 1 and 2 fail only the four LLT diagnostics; those strict failures remain preserved. This establishes a separate reference for further experiments, not full forecast qualification or a performance gain. Scope is nonperiodic CONUS Lambert with at least two physical cells per axis. Allocation-edge derivatives, polar/periodic cases and the one-layer LLT hazard remain unqualified. The separate ceiling typo is excluded.

The source correction is committed separately from performance candidates. Legacy source commit `06d4240ae989cc3e50af412bb472df3d9048783c` and all original binaries/receipts stay immutable. Roll back the correction by reverting its isolated commit. Longer, restart, holdout and full-duration qualification remain pending.

## Corrected combined qualification and restart finding

The owner-approved terrain baseline versus the combined advection row cache and RH output guard passes all seven hourly outputs for a six-hour common-domain run. Combined stock versus job-local zlib-ng also passes all seven outputs. Every scientific field, metadata item and storage-layout check is exact, and all 1,085 observed timestep records match. No field exceptions or tolerances apply. The independent October weather holdout also passes all seven hourly products (275 fields each), complete metadata/layout and all 1,085 observed timestep records. This adds held-out weather evidence without a cross-host timing claim.

The combined source/zlib-ng build also passes strict resumed-parent versus resumed-candidate comparison for the hour-2 history and restart: every field, metadata item and layout is exact, and all 180 observed timesteps match. This qualifies candidate equality along this resumed path; it does not resolve the separate baseline continuous-versus-resumed failure.

Development wall times were 1,573.280 s for the corrected parent on hpc02, 1,403.214 s for combined source on hpc04, and 1,382.705 s for combined source/zlib-ng on hpc03. Hosts, shared load and the last placement differ: these are descriptive observations, not an established stage or pipeline speedup. Performance source changes remain unpromoted while bounded matched timing is prepared.

The separate corrected continuous-versus-resumed test fails: hour-2 history has 96 variable-value failures and restart has 175. Differences include prognostic and soil state, despite all 180 logged post-checkpoint timesteps matching. Maximum diagnostic U difference is 0.182 m/s and perturbation temperature difference is 0.0688 K; this does not establish a rounding-only cause. The restored-state startup capture matches the checkpoint at all sampled physical locations. A subsequent continuous/resumed first-physics capture passes the full continuous hour-1 checkpoint integrity check and matches all 1,291,824 sampled rows bitwise through seven stages, including the first cumulus call. The known pre-adaptation clock intervals differ but the next exact timestep is 20 seconds in both runs. Five subsequent cuts also pass the full hour-1 checkpoint integrity check and match 942,480 selected rows through RK stages 1–3, microphysics and the end of the first complete 20-second solve. This is selected-column evidence. A complete physical-domain capture of 56 defined fields at the end of that first solve subsequently found 18 differing `W_2` cells near the northern boundary, on rank 12; all other 55 fields and remaining physical `W_2` cells are bitwise equal. An example differs by one raw float bit (`bcc7ef69` versus `bcc7ef6a` at i=65, k=2, j=339). The full continuous hour-1 checkpoint integrity check still passes. Halos and other state are outside this capture, and neither the cause nor its relationship to later widespread differences is established. No first-divergence repair is established. Original strict failures and the separately reviewed segment-provenance failure remain preserved. No restart qualification or numerical tolerance is claimed.

An original-source full-grid repeat also fails precisely the four LLT diagnostics in both evolved histories and both restart snapshots; initial output passes, every other field/metadata/layout check passes, and all 11 logged steps match. This evidence remains attached to the legacy reference.

## Next gates

Complete targeted restart restoration diagnosis, one bounded matched performance comparison and independent control checks for the October forcing. The full-grid original Real inputs are frozen: all 195 input and 131 boundary variables pass finite readback, with 48 boundary intervals covering 0–144 hours. It produced both expected histories and passed complete finite/layout self-readbacks; this is functional feasibility evidence, not independent trajectory qualification. All 64 rank logs verify native InfiniBand. Per-node peak memory was 28.52/24.46/24.54/24.46 GiB, with no cgroup limit/OOM events. The 128-rank, four-node control completed the same full-grid 15-minute forecast in 464.991 s, with later 49 steps averaging 2.51196 s. Initial/final history writes took 112.036/130.360 s, versus 99.226/108.172 s at 64 ranks. These are development observations with different resource counts and shared load, not an authoritative gain. Peaks were 37.09/33.42/33.33/33.32 GiB with no memory-limit/OOM events; all 128 ranks used native InfiniBand. A post-model wrapper race read a stale remote receipt after MPI exit 0. Its failed unit remains preserved; actual MPI success, final WRF completion, remote exits and empty cgroups support a separate truthful model-completion reconciliation.

Exact decomposition comparison **fails**. Initial history fields pass with separately verified integer task-count provenance, while final history has broad prognostic, soil and diagnostic value differences. All 50 printed timestep records match. Only `NTASKS_X` and `NTASKS_TOTAL` differ in metadata, as prospectively declared; output layout matches. The original strict failures and separate provenance-supplement failure are preserved. There is no tolerance, metadata normalization or scaling qualification. Further decomposition experiments are paused for diagnosis. The first checker attempt failed before array reads because the canonical checker directory lacked the adopted manifest; the already frozen pre-launch case copy was staged unchanged for the successful execution of the failing scientific comparison.

Compiled Thompson microphysics code confirms 164 static allocation/free sites in its column routine and 27 in its driver. These are static counts, not runtime frequencies or a speedup. A caller-owned workspace prototype compiles the complete original/candidate module under O0 bounds checks and strict O2; candidate column allocation sites drop to zero and the driver allocates the 164 members once per invocation. The source audit covers all members without a demonstrated stale scratch read; dynamic full-caller qualification remains required. The existing one-level sedimentation hazard is outside its 32-level scope.

The full-caller mixed rain/graupel harness instead exposes an original-code bounds defect before candidate execution: basic-mode lookup tables have third dimension 1, but consumers use physical coefficient index 5 as the storage subscript. The table creator explicitly stores that coefficient at compressed ordinal 1. Physical coefficient selection and storage ordinal must remain distinct. The original bounds failure is preserved; workspace qualification is blocked, and a separate source-backed storage-index correction passed bounded full-caller qualification. No physics option, baseline or candidate is changed to bypass the failing case. Its relationship to the restart or decomposition discrepancies is unproven.

Then advance viable candidates through longer holdout trajectories, feasible four-node full-grid execution and the full 144-hour output contract. Runtime admission uses measured memory. Authoritative timing needs matched layouts/budgets and an uncontested campaign I/O/fabric window. No full-duration or production qualification is claimed.

Public XPMEM2.7.4 source explains a plausible mechanism: `xpmem_pin_pages` temporarily binds a caller to the source task's CPU across NUMA nodes while faulting absent source PTEs, then restores its saved mask. Source presence does not prove the two old forecast events or successful restores. A new bounded plain-fork native diagnostic therefore used source CPU8/node0 and reader CPU24/node1, eight fresh unpopulated8MiB source regions, exact read/write checks, and exact reader singleton checks before/after every access. It passed16384 page accesses and64MiB full-byte validation, observed intermediate singleton8 masks concurrently, restored singleton24 after every access, and reaped its child. Invocation07b140b70a9f4ea69e52e166ee2e83cf closed success; compiler Wall/Wextra/Werror passed. This is a diagnostic, not a timing result.

The kernel compiled pin_pages inline, so a second owned native diagnostic traced actual `set_cpus_allowed_ptr` calls, restricted by cgroup/UID/reader name. Actual stacks identify `xpmem_fault_pages`/`xpmem_fault_handler`. It observed1008 accepted calls:504 switches24-to8 and504 successful restores8-to24, zero errors, empty active-call state and final phase0. The same64MiB/16384-page exact probe passed. Invocation6d437e8ee9fd4f9ea97ec677c865b2d0 closed success with no lost-event report. The initial elevated trace wrapper could not save logs on the root-squashed share; that failed scope is retained, and the corrected runner saves logs as the project user while only the tracer is elevated. Kernel modules and persistent tracing configuration were not changed.

Actual bounded DeepSeek implementation/review calls and rejected drafts are retained. Owner review rejected incorrect API/IPC/value checks in both initial probe drafts and implemented the native probe independently. A review's claim that an already obtained per-CPU pointer changes address when the task migrates was incorrect and corrected by the next review; shared-slot overwrite remains a possible source hazard, not a demonstrated bug. Another review proposed relaxing the native before/after affinity checks; that proposal is rejected because userspace resumes only after the synchronous kernel fault returns, while the independent parent observes the intermediate mask. Actual native results are not retroactive evidence for the old forecast's two untraced samples.

Disposition: XPMEM remains unpromoted; accepted sourcec2967a1b and its qualified runtime remain unchanged. Next is a narrowly traced kernel-scope qualification, retaining the original scientific checker and original rank/worker checker, followed by a small evidence-led UCX parameter screen. Installed settings include RNDV/ZCOPY thresholds, XPMEM segment size8256, FIFO256 and maximum RX poll16; listing a setting does not establish benefit. Separate parallel streams own trace-scope integration and message-size-based knob selection. ## Serial-tile diagnostic rejected by exact AFWA output (2026-10-16)

The fixed two-versus-one serial-tile diagnostic used the same current `1e2c6e9a` executable, provider `375aa7e3`, codec `881d0a1a`, 512-rank decomposition and scientific settings. Both arms completed all 35 ordered steps through 06:10; their initial 06:00 products matched exactly. The one-tile candidate is rejected and the two-tile default is retained. No timing or promotion claim follows. The checker retained eight first mismatch examples, not a total differing-cell count. One FZRA example at [0,634,34] was approximately halved (1.18474543e-6 versus 5.92372771e-7), a substantive numerical change.

A bounded source-only inspection found that the diagnostics driver calls AFWA once per tile, but precipitation-type diagnostics accumulate persistent buckets over unchanged patch bounds on every call. That call structure explains the approximate factor-of-two precipitation examples. LLWS additionally resets the entire memory region on an after-history timestep before updating only the current tile; repeated tile calls can erase earlier-tile maxima. This establishes tile-sensitive source structure, while attributing every observed LLWS difference to that reset remains an inference. No scientific repair or baseline change was attempted.

The first frozen attempt failed before WRF started because a stale profiled remote adapter required profiling authorization. It remains failed. A fresh namespace reused the qualified unprofiled adapter plus the existing tile-environment observations, then reached the strict scientific failure above. ### Existing-trace attribution: separation accumulates between first gathers

A bounded metadata-only analysis reused both exact-qualified traces and their nine causally bracketed cohorts, checking 8,640 rank rows. For each host, signed current-entry offset equals signed previous-exit offset plus the difference in local inter-gather elapsed time (`A=P+D`), using only same-clock timestamps. In the compression-free window's four long-call cohorts, host arrival spreads were **144.73–158.70 ms**, while previous-exit spread was at most **0.326 ms**. The earlier output-overlap sample similarly showed **244.09–319.30 ms** entry spread versus at most **2.339 ms** prior-exit spread. The observed separation was therefore accumulated between these gather calls, rather than already present at the preceding departure.

That intervening interval includes the rest of the step, other MPI calls/spinning, scheduling and computation. On hpc01, locally late ranks 384/416 sometimes used less thread CPU than the fixed host reference despite additional elapsed time; other hosts' thread CPU was close to elapsed. This does not identify pure physics, a network fault or removable headroom. It directs the next measured attribution toward intervening work rather than another first-gather API-only microtest. Two actual independent DeepSeek method reviews returned and their scope cautions were applied; they did not certify the local implementation. Result `b56a5fe0`, positive closure `76918474`, summary `03cc3283`, campaign `inter-gather-gap-method-r1/`. All analysis scopes closed. Initial missing remote-closure binding and unsupported optional `memory.peak` reporting failures remain preserved; the final result retains original trace checks and reports the unavailable counter as null.
